  +fn try_step_stderr_empty(
  +    step_text: &str,
  +    world: &mut World,
  +    _example: &Example,
  +) -> Option<StepResult> {
  +    if step_text != "stderr is empty" {
  +        return None;
  +    }
  +    let stderr = match &world.stderr {
  +        Some(s) => s.clone(),
  +        None => return Some(StepResult::fail("stderr not yet recorded")),
  +    };
  +    Some(if stderr.is_empty() {
  +        StepResult::ok()
  +    } else {
  +        StepResult::fail(format!("expected empty stderr, got: {:?}", stderr))
  +    })
  +}
  +
  +fn try_step_stderr_no_panic(
  +    step_text: &str,
  +    world: &mut World,
  +    _example: &Example,
  +) -> Option<StepResult> {
  +    if step_text != "stderr contains no panic text" {
  +        return None;
  +    }
  +    let stderr = match &world.stderr {
  +        Some(s) => s.clone(),
  +        None => return Some(StepResult::fail("stderr not yet recorded")),
  +    };
  +    Some(if stderr.contains("panicked at") || stderr.contains("stack backtrace") {
  +        StepResult::fail(format!("stderr contains panic text: {:?}", stderr))
  +    } else {
  +        StepResult::ok()
  +    })

  +    if let Err(e) = std::fs::write(&lib_path, "pub fn answer() -> i32 { 42 }\n") {
  +        return Some(StepResult::fail(format!("failed to write lib.rs: {}", e)));
  +    }
  +    Some(StepResult::ok())

  +struct Cli {
  +    #[arg(help = "Paths to scan (files or directories)")]
  +    paths: Vec<String>,
  +
  +    #[arg(long, default_value = "0.82", help = "Jaccard similarity threshold")]
  +    threshold: f64,
  +
  +    #[arg(long, default_value = "4", help = "Minimum source lines")]
  +    min_lines: usize,
  +
  +    #[arg(long, default_value = "20", help = "Minimum normalized AST nodes")]

  +
  +    #[arg(long, default_value = "text", help = "Output format: text or json")]
  +    format: String,
  +
  +    #[arg(long, help = "Force language (only 'rust' supported)")]
  +    lang: Option<String>,
  +
  +    #[arg(long, help = "Exclude glob patterns (repeatable)")]
  +    exclude: Vec<String>,
  +}
  +
  +fn main() {
  +    let cli = Cli::parse();
  +
  +    let format = match cli.format.as_str() {
  +        "json" => OutputFormat::Json,
  +        "text" => OutputFormat::Text,
  +        other => {
  +            eprintln!("error: unknown format '{}'; use 'text' or 'json'", other);
  +            process::exit(2);
  +        }
  +    };
  +
  +    if let Some(lang) = &cli.lang {
  +        if lang != "rust" {
  +            eprintln!("error: unsupported language '{}'; only 'rust' is supported", lang);
  +            process::exit(2);
  +        }
  +    }
  +
  +    let config = Config {
  +        threshold: cli.threshold,
  +        min_lines: cli.min_lines,
  +        min_nodes: cli.min_nodes,
  +        format,
  +        excludes: cli.exclude,
  +    };
  +
  +    match run(&cli.paths, &config) {
  +        RunResult::Clean => {
  +            if matches!(config.format, OutputFormat::Json) {
  +                println!("[]");
  +            }
  +            process::exit(0);
  +        }
  +        RunResult::Duplicates(pairs) => {
  +            match config.format {
  +                OutputFormat::Text => print!("{}", format_text(&pairs)),
  +                OutputFormat::Json => println!("{}", format_json(&pairs)),
  +            }
  +            process::exit(1);
  +        }
  +        RunResult::Error(msg) => {
  +            eprintln!("error: {}", msg);
  +            process::exit(2);
  +        }
  +    }
  +}

1	use crate::runtime::{Example, StepResult, World};
2	use std::process::Command;
3	use std::sync::OnceLock;
4	
5	pub fn dispatch(step_text: &str, world: &mut World, example: &Example) -> StepResult {
6	    if try_step_given_binary_built(step_text, world, example).is_some() {
7	        return StepResult::ok();
8	    }
9	    if let Some(result) = try_step_minimal_rust_dir(step_text, world, example) {
10	        return result;
11	    }
12	    if let Some(result) = try_step_run_with_args(step_text, world, example) {
13	        return result;
14	    }
15	    if let Some(result) = try_step_exit_code(step_text, world, example) {
16	        return result;
17	    }
18	    if let Some(result) = try_step_stdout_empty(step_text, world, example) {
19	        return result;
20	    }
21	    if let Some(result) = try_step_stderr_empty(step_text, world, example) {
22	        return result;
23	    }
24	    if let Some(result) = try_step_stderr_no_panic(step_text, world, example) {
25	        return result;
26	    }
27	    StepResult::fail(format!("unsupported step: {}", step_text))
28	}
29	
30	fn try_step_given_binary_built(
31	    step_text: &str,
32	    world: &mut World,
33	    _example: &Example,
34	) -> Option<()> {
35	    static PATTERN: &str = "the drywall release binary is built";
36	    if step_text != PATTERN {
37	        return None;
38	    }
39	    let path = std::env::var("DRYWALL_BINARY")
40	        .unwrap_or_else(|_| "./target/release/drywall".to_string());
41	    world.binary_path = Some(path);
42	    Some(())
43	}
44	
45	fn step_run_with_args_re() -> &'static regex::Regex {
46	    static RE: OnceLock<regex::Regex> = OnceLock::new();
47	    RE.get_or_init(|| regex::Regex::new(r#"^the binary is run with the arguments "([^"]*)"$"#).unwrap())
48	}
49	
50	fn example_param_re() -> &'static regex::Regex {
51	    static RE: OnceLock<regex::Regex> = OnceLock::new();
52	    RE.get_or_init(|| regex::Regex::new(r"^<([A-Za-z0-9_]+)>$").unwrap())
53	}
54	
55	fn resolve_args_string(raw: &str, example: &Example) -> Result<String, StepResult> {
56	    if let Some(c) = example_param_re().captures(raw) {
57	        let key = c.get(1).unwrap().as_str();
58	        example
59	            .get(key)
60	            .cloned()
61	            .ok_or_else(|| StepResult::fail(format!("missing example key: {}", key)))
62	    } else {
63	        Ok(raw.to_string())
64	    }
65	}
66	
67	fn run_binary(binary: &str, args_str: &str, world: &mut World) -> StepResult {
68	    let args: Vec<&str> = if args_str.is_empty() {
69	        vec![]
70	    } else {
71	        args_str.split_whitespace().collect()
72	    };
73	    match Command::new(binary).args(&args).output() {
74	        Ok(o) => {
75	            world.exit_code = Some(o.status.code().unwrap_or(-1));
76	            world.stdout = Some(String::from_utf8_lossy(&o.stdout).to_string());
77	            world.stderr = Some(String::from_utf8_lossy(&o.stderr).to_string());
78	            StepResult::ok()
79	        }
80	        Err(e) => StepResult::fail(format!("failed to run binary: {}", e)),
81	    }
82	}
83	
84	fn try_step_run_with_args(
85	    step_text: &str,
86	    world: &mut World,
87	    example: &Example,
88	) -> Option<StepResult> {
89	    let caps = step_run_with_args_re().captures(step_text)?;
90	    let raw_args = caps.get(1).map_or("", |m| m.as_str());

7	pub fn parse_rust_source(source: &str) -> bool {
8	    let mut parser = tree_sitter::Parser::new();
9	    parser
10	        .set_language(&tree_sitter_rust::LANGUAGE.into())
11	        .expect("failed to load Rust grammar");
12	    let tree = parser.parse(source, None).expect("parse returned None");
13	    !tree.root_node().has_error()
14	}

44	    pub excludes: Vec<String>,
45	}
46	
47	impl Default for Config {
48	    fn default() -> Self {
49	        Config {
50	            threshold: 0.82,
51	            min_lines: 4,
52	            min_nodes: 20,
53	            format: OutputFormat::Text,
54	            excludes: vec![],
55	        }
56	    }
57	}
58	
59	pub enum OutputFormat {
60	    Text,
61	    Json,
62	}
63	
64	pub enum RunResult {
65	    Clean,
66	    Duplicates(Vec<DuplicatePair>),
67	    Error(String),
68	}
69	
70	pub fn run(paths: &[String], config: &Config) -> RunResult {
71	    let exclude_set = match build_glob_set(&config.excludes) {
72	        Ok(gs) => gs,
73	        Err(e) => return RunResult::Error(format!("invalid exclude glob: {}", e)),
74	    };
75	
76	    let mut functions: Vec<FunctionInfo> = Vec::new();
77	    let mut errors: Vec<String> = Vec::new();
78	
79	    for path_str in paths {
80	        let path = Path::new(path_str);
81	        if !path.exists() {
82	            errors.push(format!("path does not exist: {}", path_str));
83	            continue;
84	        }
85	        collect_functions_from_path(path, &exclude_set, &mut functions, &mut errors);
86	    }
87	
88	    if !errors.is_empty() {
89	        return RunResult::Error(errors.join("\n"));
90	    }
91	
92	    let pairs = find_duplicate_pairs(
93	        &functions,
94	        config.threshold,
95	        config.min_lines,
96	        config.min_nodes,
97	    );
98	
99	    if pairs.is_empty() {
100	        RunResult::Clean
101	    } else {
102	        RunResult::Duplicates(pairs)
103	    }
104	}
105	
106	fn build_glob_set(patterns: &[String]) -> Result<GlobSet, globset::Error> {
107	    let mut builder = GlobSetBuilder::new();
108	    for pat in patterns {
109	        builder.add(Glob::new(pat)?);
110	    }
111	    builder.build()
112	}
113	
114	fn collect_functions_from_path(
115	    path: &Path,
116	    exclude_set: &GlobSet,
117	    functions: &mut Vec<FunctionInfo>,
118	    errors: &mut Vec<String>,
119	) {
120	    if path.is_file() {
121	        if should_skip(path, exclude_set) {
122	            return;
123	        }
124	        if path.extension().and_then(|e| e.to_str()) == Some("rs") {
125	            process_file(path, functions, errors);
126	        }
127	    } else {
128	        for entry in WalkDir::new(path).sort_by_file_name() {
129	            match entry {
130	                Ok(e) => {
131	                    let ep = e.path();
132	                    if should_skip(ep, exclude_set) {
133	                        continue;
134	                    }
135	                    if e.file_type().is_file()
136	                        && ep.extension().and_then(|x| x.to_str()) == Some("rs")
137	                    {
138	                        process_file(ep, functions, errors);
139	                    }
140	                }
141	                Err(err) => {
142	                    errors.push(format!("walk error: {}", err));
143	                }
144	            }
145	        }
146	    }
147	}
148	
149	fn should_skip(path: &Path, exclude_set: &GlobSet) -> bool {
150	    if exclude_set.is_empty() {
151	        return false;
152	    }
153	    exclude_set.is_match(path)
154	}
155	
156	fn process_file(path: &Path, functions: &mut Vec<FunctionInfo>, errors: &mut Vec<String>) {
157	    let source = match std::fs::read_to_string(path) {
158	        Ok(s) => s,
159	        Err(e) => {
160	            errors.push(format!("cannot read {}: {}", path.display(), e));
161	            return;
162	        }
163	    };
164	
165	    let mut parser = tree_sitter::Parser::new();
166	    parser
167	        .set_language(&tree_sitter_rust::LANGUAGE.into())
168	        .expect("failed to load Rust grammar");
169	
170	    let tree = match parser.parse(&source, None) {
171	        Some(t) => t,
172	        None => {
173	            errors.push(format!("parse returned None for {}", path.display()));
174	            return;
175	        }
176	    };
177	
178	    if tree.root_node().has_error() {
179	        errors.push(format!("parse error in {}", path.display()));
180	        return;
181	    }
182	
183	    let file_str = path.to_string_lossy().to_string();
184	    extract_functions(tree.root_node(), &source, &file_str, functions);
185	}
186	
187	fn extract_functions(
188	    node: tree_sitter::Node,
189	    source: &str,
190	    file: &str,
191	    functions: &mut Vec<FunctionInfo>,
192	) {
193	    match node.kind() {
194	        "function_item" => {
195	            let info = make_function_info(node, source, file);
196	            functions.push(info);
197	        }
198	        "let_declaration" => {
199	            if let Some(val) = find_child_by_field(node, "value") {
200	                if val.kind() == "closure_expression" {
201	                    let info = make_function_info(node, source, file);
202	                    functions.push(info);
203	                }
204	            }
205	            let mut cursor = node.walk();
206	            let children: Vec<_> = node.children(&mut cursor).collect();
207	            for child in children {
208	                if child.kind() != "closure_expression" {
209	                    extract_functions(child, source, file, functions);
210	                }
211	            }
212	        }
213	        _ => {
214	            let mut cursor = node.walk();
215	            let children: Vec<_> = node.children(&mut cursor).collect();
216	            for child in children {
217	                extract_functions(child, source, file, functions);
218	            }
219	        }
220	    }
221	}
222	
223	fn find_child_by_field<'a>(
224	    node: tree_sitter::Node<'a>,
225	    field: &str,
226	) -> Option<tree_sitter::Node<'a>> {
227	    let mut cursor = node.walk();
228	    for child in node.children(&mut cursor) {
229	        if child.kind() == field {
230	            return Some(child);
231	        }
232	    }
233	    node.child_by_field_name(field)
234	}
235	
236	fn make_function_info(node: tree_sitter::Node, source: &str, file: &str) -> FunctionInfo {
237	    let start_line = node.start_position().row + 1;
238	    let end_line = node.end_position().row + 1;
239	    let hashes = collect_subtree_hashes(node, source);
240	    FunctionInfo {
241	        file: file.to_string(),
242	        start_line,
243	        end_line,
244	        node_hashes: hashes,
245	    }
246	}
247	
248	fn normalize_node_text(node: tree_sitter::Node, source: &str) -> String {
249	    match node.kind() {
250	        "identifier" | "field_identifier" | "type_identifier" | "lifetime" => "_ID".to_string(),
251	        "integer_literal" | "float_literal" | "string_literal" | "raw_string_literal"
252	        | "char_literal" | "boolean_literal" => "_LIT".to_string(),
253	        _ => {
254	            let start = node.start_byte();
255	            let end = node.end_byte();
256	            source[start..end].to_string()
257	        }
258	    }
259	}
260	
261	fn build_normalized_subtree(node: tree_sitter::Node, source: &str) -> String {
262	    if node.child_count() == 0 {
263	        return normalize_node_text(node, source);
264	    }
265	    let mut parts = vec![format!("({}:", node.kind())];
266	    let mut cursor = node.walk();
267	    for child in node.children(&mut cursor) {
268	        parts.push(build_normalized_subtree(child, source));
269	    }
270	    parts.push(")".to_string());
271	    parts.join("")
272	}
273	
274	fn hash_str(s: &str) -> u64 {
275	    use std::hash::{Hash, Hasher};
276	    let mut h = std::collections::hash_map::DefaultHasher::new();
277	    s.hash(&mut h);
278	    h.finish()
279	}
280	
281	fn collect_subtree_hashes(node: tree_sitter::Node, source: &str) -> Vec<u64> {
282	    let mut hashes = Vec::new();
283	    collect_hashes_recursive(node, source, &mut hashes);
284	    hashes.sort_unstable();
285	    hashes
286	}
287	
288	fn collect_hashes_recursive(node: tree_sitter::Node, source: &str, hashes: &mut Vec<u64>) {
289	    let subtree = build_normalized_subtree(node, source);
290	    hashes.push(hash_str(&subtree));
291	    let mut cursor = node.walk();
292	    for child in node.children(&mut cursor) {
293	        collect_hashes_recursive(child, source, hashes);
294	    }
295	}
296	
297	fn jaccard(a: &[u64], b: &[u64]) -> f64 {
298	    if a.is_empty() && b.is_empty() {
299	        return 1.0;
300	    }
301	    let set_a: HashSet<u64> = a.iter().copied().collect();
302	    let set_b: HashSet<u64> = b.iter().copied().collect();
303	    let intersection = set_a.intersection(&set_b).count();
304	    let union = set_a.union(&set_b).count();
305	    if union == 0 {
306	        return 0.0;
307	    }
308	    intersection as f64 / union as f64
309	}
310	
311	fn source_lines(fi: &FunctionInfo) -> usize {
312	    fi.end_line.saturating_sub(fi.start_line) + 1
313	}
314	
315	fn find_duplicate_pairs(
316	    functions: &[FunctionInfo],
317	    threshold: f64,
318	    min_lines: usize,
319	    min_nodes: usize,
320	) -> Vec<DuplicatePair> {
321	    let candidates: Vec<&FunctionInfo> = functions
322	        .iter()
323	        .filter(|f| source_lines(f) >= min_lines && f.node_hashes.len() >= min_nodes)
324	        .collect();
325	
326	    let mut pairs: Vec<DuplicatePair> = Vec::new();
327	
328	    for i in 0..candidates.len() {
329	        for j in (i + 1)..candidates.len() {
330	            let a = candidates[i];
331	            let b = candidates[j];
332	            if a.file == b.file && a.start_line == b.start_line {
333	                continue;
334	            }
335	            let score = jaccard(&a.node_hashes, &b.node_hashes);
336	            if score >= threshold {
337	                let (left, right) = order_endpoints(a, b);
338	                pairs.push(DuplicatePair {
339	                    score,
340	                    left: PairEndpoint {
341	                        file: left.file.clone(),
342	                        start_line: left.start_line,
343	                        end_line: left.end_line,
344	                        nodes: left.node_hashes.len(),
345	                    },
346	                    right: PairEndpoint {
347	                        file: right.file.clone(),
348	                        start_line: right.start_line,
349	                        end_line: right.end_line,
350	                        nodes: right.node_hashes.len(),
351	                    },
352	                });
353	            }
354	        }
355	    }
356	
357	    pairs.sort_by(|a, b| {
358	        b.score
359	            .partial_cmp(&a.score)
360	            .unwrap_or(std::cmp::Ordering::Equal)
361	            .then_with(|| a.left.file.cmp(&b.left.file))
362	            .then_with(|| a.left.start_line.cmp(&b.left.start_line))
363	            .then_with(|| a.right.file.cmp(&b.right.file))
364	            .then_with(|| a.right.start_line.cmp(&b.right.start_line))
365	    });
366	
367	    pairs
368	}
369	
370	fn order_endpoints<'a>(
371	    a: &'a FunctionInfo,
372	    b: &'a FunctionInfo,
373	) -> (&'a FunctionInfo, &'a FunctionInfo) {
374	    let cmp = a
375	        .file
376	        .cmp(&b.file)
377	        .then_with(|| a.start_line.cmp(&b.start_line));
378	    if cmp == std::cmp::Ordering::Greater {
379	        (b, a)
380	    } else {
381	        (a, b)
382	    }
383	}
384	
385	pub fn format_text(pairs: &[DuplicatePair]) -> String {
386	    let mut out = String::new();
387	    for p in pairs {
388	        out.push_str(&format!("DUPLICATE score={:.4}\n", p.score));
389	        out.push_str(&format!(
390	            "  {}:{}-{}\n",
391	            p.left.file, p.left.start_line, p.left.end_line
392	        ));
393	        out.push_str(&format!(
394	            "  {}:{}-{}  ({} nodes / {} nodes)\n",
395	            p.right.file, p.right.start_line, p.right.end_line, p.left.nodes, p.right.nodes
396	        ));
397	    }
398	    out
399	}
400	
401	pub fn format_json(pairs: &[DuplicatePair]) -> String {
402	    serde_json::to_string_pretty(pairs).unwrap_or_else(|_| "[]".to_string())
403	}
404	
405	pub fn collect_rust_files(paths: &[String]) -> Vec<PathBuf> {
406	    let mut files = Vec::new();
407	    for p in paths {
408	        let path = Path::new(p);
409	        if path.is_file() {
410	            files.push(path.to_path_buf());
411	        } else {
412	            for entry in WalkDir::new(path).sort_by_file_name() {
413	                if let Ok(e) = entry {
414	                    if e.file_type().is_file()
415	                        && e.path().extension().and_then(|x| x.to_str()) == Some("rs")
416	                    {
417	                        files.push(e.path().to_path_buf());
418	                    }
419	                }
420	            }
421	        }
422	    }
423	    files
424	}

  "new_string": "pub fn run(paths: &[String], config: &Config) -> RunResult {\n    let exclude_set = match build_glob_set(&config.excludes) {\n        Ok(gs) => gs,\n        Err(e) => return RunResult::Error(format!(\"invalid exclude glob: {}\", e)),\n    };\n\n    let (functions, errors) = collect_all_functions(paths, &exclude_set);\n\n    if !errors.is_empty() {\n        return RunResult::Error(errors.join(\"\\n\"));\n    }\n\n    let pairs = find_duplicate_pairs(\n        &functions,\n        config.threshold,\n        config.min_lines,\n        config.min_nodes,\n    );\n\n    if pairs.is_empty() {\n        RunResult::Clean\n    } else {\n        RunResult::Duplicates(pairs)\n    }\n}\n\nfn collect_all_functions(\n    paths: &[String],\n    exclude_set: &GlobSet,\n) -> (Vec<FunctionInfo>, Vec<String>) {\n    let mut functions: Vec<FunctionInfo> = Vec::new();\n    let mut errors: Vec<String> = Vec::new();\n    for path_str in paths {\n        let path = Path::new(path_str);\n        if !path.exists() {\n            errors.push(format!(\"path does not exist: {}\", path_str));\n        } else {\n            collect_functions_from_path(path, exclude_set, &mut functions, &mut errors);\n        }\n    }\n    (functions, errors)\n}"
}

  "new_string": "fn make_parser() -> tree_sitter::Parser {\n    let mut parser = tree_sitter::Parser::new();\n    parser\n        .set_language(&tree_sitter_rust::LANGUAGE.into())\n        .expect(\"failed to load Rust grammar\");\n    parser\n}\n\nfn parse_source_tree(source: &str) -> Result<tree_sitter::Tree, &'static str> {\n    let tree = make_parser().parse(source, None).ok_or(\"parse returned None\")?;\n    if tree.root_node().has_error() {\n        return Err(\"parse error\");\n    }\n    Ok(tree)\n}\n\nfn process_file(path: &Path, functions: &mut Vec<FunctionInfo>, errors: &mut Vec<String>) {\n    let source = match std::fs::read_to_string(path) {\n        Ok(s) => s,\n        Err(e) => {\n            errors.push(format!(\"cannot read {}: {}\", path.display(), e));\n            return;\n        }\n    };\n\n    match parse_source_tree(&source) {\n        Ok(tree) => {\n            let file_str = path.to_string_lossy().to_string();\n            extract_functions(tree.root_node(), &source, &file_str, functions);\n        }\n        Err(msg) => {\n            errors.push(format!(\"{} in {}\", msg, path.display()));\n        }\n    }\n}"

> AGENT

Good progress — down from 9 to 4 failing. Remaining:
- `main` (CRAP 156) — boundary, intentionally uncovered
- `extract_functions` (CRAP 12.7, CC=9, 64.3%) — needs either coverage boost or CC reduction
- `collect_functions_from_path` (CRAP 10.1, CC=10, 89.7%) — needs CC reduction (already at 89.7%)
- `find_duplicate_pairs` (CRAP 6.0, CC=6, 97.9%) — exactly at threshold, need CRAP < 6

For `main`: it's an environmental boundary. The CRAP tool counts it. But the role says "Move behavior out of environmentally unsuitable modules into testable modules." The `main` function is the boundary shell — its logic already calls into `drywall::run`. However it still has CC=12. The format parsing and lang validation could be extracted to a testable function in `lib.rs`.

For `extract_functions` (CC=9, 64.3%): I need to get to 100% coverage or reduce CC significantly. Let me check what's missing.

For `collect_functions_from_path` (CC=10, 89.7%): at 90%+ the CRAP gets closer. CRAP = CC^2 * (1-c)^3 + CC. At 100%: CRAP=CC. At 89.7%: 100*(0.103)^3 + 10 = 10.11. To get below 6, need CC≤5 or coverage > ~98%.

Let me focus on reducing CC in `collect_functions_from_path` and `extract_functions`, and also handle `main`.

  "new_string": "fn is_rust_file(path: &Path) -> bool {\n    path.extension().and_then(|e| e.to_str()) == Some(\"rs\")\n}\n\nfn collect_functions_from_path(\n    path: &Path,\n    exclude_set: &GlobSet,\n    functions: &mut Vec<FunctionInfo>,\n    errors: &mut Vec<String>,\n) {\n    if path.is_file() {\n        collect_from_single_file(path, exclude_set, functions, errors);\n    } else {\n        collect_from_directory(path, exclude_set, functions, errors);\n    }\n}\n\nfn collect_from_single_file(\n    path: &Path,\n    exclude_set: &GlobSet,\n    functions: &mut Vec<FunctionInfo>,\n    errors: &mut Vec<String>,\n) {\n    if !should_skip(path, exclude_set) && is_rust_file(path) {\n        process_file(path, functions, errors);\n    }\n}\n\nfn collect_from_directory(\n    path: &Path,\n    exclude_set: &GlobSet,\n    functions: &mut Vec<FunctionInfo>,\n    errors: &mut Vec<String>,\n) {\n    for entry in WalkDir::new(path).sort_by_file_name() {\n        match entry {\n            Ok(e) => {\n                let ep = e.path();\n                if !should_skip(ep, exclude_set) && e.file_type().is_file() && is_rust_file(ep) {\n                    process_file(ep, functions, errors);\n                }\n            }\n            Err(err) => {\n                errors.push(format!(\"walk error: {}\", err));\n            }\n        }\n    }\n}"
}

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/gabadi/workspace/addi/drywall/.worktrees/cleaner/src/lib.rs",
  "old_string": "fn extract_functions(\n    node: tree_sitter::Node,\n    source: &str,\n    file: &str,\n    functions: &mut Vec<FunctionInfo>,\n) {\n    match node.kind() {\n        \"function_item\" => {\n            let info = make_function_info(node, source, file);\n            functions.push(info);\n        }\n        \"let_declaration\" => {\n            if let Some(val) = find_child_by_field(node, \"value\") && val.kind() == \"closure_expression\" {\n                let info = make_function_info(node, source, file);\n                functions.push(info);\n            }\n            let mut cursor = node.walk();\n            let children: Vec<_> = node.children(&mut cursor).collect();\n            for child in children {\n                if child.kind() != \"closure_expression\" {\n                    extract_functions(child, source, file, functions);\n                }\n            }\n        }\n        _ => {\n            let mut cursor = node.walk();\n            let children: Vec<_> = node.children(&mut cursor).collect();\n            for child in children {\n                extract_functions(child, source, file, functions);\n            }\n        }\n    }\n}",
  "new_string": "fn children_of(node: tree_sitter::Node) -> Vec<tree_sitter::Node> {\n    let mut cursor = node.walk();\n    node.children(&mut cursor).collect()\n}\n\nfn extract_functions(\n    node: tree_sitter::Node,\n    source: &str,\n    file: &str,\n    functions: &mut Vec<FunctionInfo>,\n) {\n    match node.kind() {\n        \"function_item\" => {\n            functions.push(make_function_info(node, source, file));\n        }\n        \"let_declaration\" => {\n            extract_let_declaration(node, source, file, functions);\n        }\n        _ => {\n            for child in children_of(node) {\n                extract_functions(child, source, file, functions);\n            }\n        }\n    }\n}\n\nfn extract_let_declaration(\n    node: tree_sitter::Node,\n    source: &str,\n    file: &str,\n    functions: &mut Vec<FunctionInfo>,\n) {\n    if let Some(val) = find_child_by_field(node, \"value\") && val.kind() == \"closure_expression\" {\n        functions.push(make_function_info(node, source, file));\n    }\n    for child in children_of(node) {\n        if child.kind() != \"closure_expression\" {\n            extract_functions(child, source, file, functions);\n        }\n    }\n}"
}

}

Hmm, `extract_let_declaration` has 0% coverage but CC=5 (CRAP=30). That's because the coverage test is calling `extract_functions` which dispatches to it, but the coverage tool isn't tracking the call. Let me check why.

Actually, I see — the existing test `extract_functions_finds_closure_in_let` does cover the `let_declaration` path. Let me check if coverage is picking it up.

Wait — CRAP 30 with CC=5 and 0% means it's not covered at all. Let me verify the tests actually exercise this path.

Actually, looking at this more carefully: the refactoring moved the let_declaration logic to `extract_let_declaration`, but the existing test source uses a closure inside a function body. Let me check if that actually hits `let_declaration` in tree-sitter.

The issue is `let_declaration` is tree-sitter's node kind for `let x = ...` statements. A closure assigned to a let binding would have a `let_declaration` parent. But closures in Rust are `closure_expression` nodes. My test `extract_functions_finds_closure_in_let` does: `let f = |x| { ... }` — this creates a `let_declaration` with a `closure_expression` value.

But coverage at 0% means the test isn't actually calling the extracted function. Let me verify the test is actually running and the function is reachable.

Actually wait — the CRAP tool might not be seeing the same coverage data. The lcov.info was generated after the tests passed. Let me check if the function name appears in the lcov file.

> AGENT

The function is defined but never called in coverage. That's strange since the test calls `extract_functions` which should call `extract_let_declaration`. Let me check the actual test:

The test `extract_functions_finds_closure_in_let` uses source with `fn outer() { let f = |x| {...}; }`. This would parse as: `source_file -> function_item -> block -> let_declaration`. When `extract_functions` processes `function_item`, it adds it as a function and stops (doesn't recurse into function_item). So it never processes the children with `let_declaration`.

Actually looking at the code — `"function_item"` arm just adds the function info and **doesn't recurse**. Only `"let_declaration"` and `"_"` recurse. So a `let` inside a function body would only be processed if it's at the top level (not inside a `function_item`).

This means the `let_declaration` code is for top-level `const/let` bindings with closures. Let me write a test for top-level let with closure:

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/gabadi/workspace/addi/drywall/.worktrees/cleaner/src/lib.rs",
  "old_string": "fn collect_from_directory(\n    path: &Path,\n    exclude_set: &GlobSet,\n    functions: &mut Vec<FunctionInfo>,\n    errors: &mut Vec<String>,\n) {\n    for entry in WalkDir::new(path).sort_by_file_name() {\n        match entry {\n            Ok(e) => {\n                let ep = e.path();\n                if !should_skip(ep, exclude_set) && e.file_type().is_file() && is_rust_file(ep) {\n                    process_file(ep, functions, errors);\n                }\n            }\n            Err(err) => {\n                errors.push(format!(\"walk error: {}\", err));\n            }\n        }\n    }\n}",
  "new_string": "fn should_process_entry(e: &walkdir::DirEntry, exclude_set: &GlobSet) -> bool {\n    let ep = e.path();\n    !should_skip(ep, exclude_set) && e.file_type().is_file() && is_rust_file(ep)\n}\n\nfn collect_from_directory(\n    path: &Path,\n    exclude_set: &GlobSet,\n    functions: &mut Vec<FunctionInfo>,\n    errors: &mut Vec<String>,\n) {\n    for entry in WalkDir::new(path).sort_by_file_name() {\n        match entry {\n            Ok(e) if should_process_entry(&e, exclude_set) => {\n                process_file(e.path(), functions, errors);\n            }\n            Ok(_) => {}\n            Err(err) => {\n                errors.push(format!(\"walk error: {}\", err));\n            }\n        }\n    }\n}"
}

  "old_string": "fn find_duplicate_pairs(\n    functions: &[FunctionInfo],\n    threshold: f64,\n    min_lines: usize,\n    min_nodes: usize,\n) -> Vec<DuplicatePair> {\n    let candidates: Vec<&FunctionInfo> = functions\n        .iter()\n        .filter(|f| source_lines(f) >= min_lines && f.node_hashes.len() >= min_nodes)\n        .collect();\n\n    let mut pairs: Vec<DuplicatePair> = Vec::new();\n\n    for i in 0..candidates.len() {\n        for j in (i + 1)..candidates.len() {\n            let a = candidates[i];\n            let b = candidates[j];\n            if a.file == b.file && a.start_line == b.start_line {\n                continue;\n            }\n            let score = jaccard(&a.node_hashes, &b.node_hashes);\n            if score >= threshold {\n                let (left, right) = order_endpoints(a, b);\n                pairs.push(DuplicatePair {\n                    score,\n                    left: PairEndpoint {\n                        file: left.file.clone(),\n                        start_line: left.start_line,\n                        end_line: left.end_line,\n                        nodes: left.node_hashes.len(),\n                    },\n                    right: PairEndpoint {\n                        file: right.file.clone(),\n                        start_line: right.start_line,\n                        end_line: right.end_line,\n                        nodes: right.node_hashes.len(),\n                    },\n                });\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    pairs.sort_by(|a, b| {\n        b.score\n            .partial_cmp(&a.score)\n            .unwrap_or(std::cmp::Ordering::Equal)\n            .then_with(|| a.left.file.cmp(&b.left.file))\n            .then_with(|| a.left.start_line.cmp(&b.left.start_line))\n            .then_with(|| a.right.file.cmp(&b.right.file))\n            .then_with(|| a.right.start_line.cmp(&b.right.start_line))\n    });\n\n    pairs\n}",
  "new_string": "fn make_pair(a: &FunctionInfo, b: &FunctionInfo, score: f64) -> DuplicatePair {\n    let (left, right) = order_endpoints(a, b);\n    DuplicatePair {\n        score,\n        left: PairEndpoint {\n            file: left.file.clone(),\n            start_line: left.start_line,\n            end_line: left.end_line,\n            nodes: left.node_hashes.len(),\n        },\n        right: PairEndpoint {\n            file: right.file.clone(),\n            start_line: right.start_line,\n            end_line: right.end_line,\n            nodes: right.node_hashes.len(),\n        },\n    }\n}\n\nfn is_same_location(a: &FunctionInfo, b: &FunctionInfo) -> bool {\n    a.file == b.file && a.start_line == b.start_line\n}\n\nfn find_duplicate_pairs(\n    functions: &[FunctionInfo],\n    threshold: f64,\n    min_lines: usize,\n    min_nodes: usize,\n) -> Vec<DuplicatePair> {\n    let candidates: Vec<&FunctionInfo> = functions\n        .iter()\n        .filter(|f| source_lines(f) >= min_lines && f.node_hashes.len() >= min_nodes)\n        .collect();\n\n    let mut pairs: Vec<DuplicatePair> = Vec::new();\n\n    for i in 0..candidates.len() {\n        for j in (i + 1)..candidates.len() {\n            let a = candidates[i];\n            let b = candidates[j];\n            if is_same_location(a, b) {\n                continue;\n            }\n            let score = jaccard(&a.node_hashes, &b.node_hashes);\n            if score >= threshold {\n                pairs.push(make_pair(a, b, score));\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    pairs.sort_by(|a, b| {\n        b.score\n            .partial_cmp(&a.score)\n            .unwrap_or(std::cmp::Ordering::Equal)\n            .then_with(|| a.left.file.cmp(&b.left.file))\n            .then_with(|| a.left.start_line.cmp(&b.left.start_line))\n            .then_with(|| a.right.file.cmp(&b.right.file))\n            .then_with(|| a.right.start_line.cmp(&b.right.start_line))\n    });\n\n    pairs\n}"
}

> AGENT

Only `main` fails now. `main` is the boundary adapter — it calls `process::exit()` and reads from `Cli::parse()` which does I/O. Per the constitution: "Move behavior out of environmentally unsuitable modules into testable modules when that can be done without changing behavior. Keep unsuitable modules as small adapter shells excluded from tools that run tests."

The `main` binary is excluded from the coverage command via `--profile unit` — but it still appears in CRAP. The AGENTS.md says `--exclude 'acceptance/**'` for CRAP. The `main.rs` should also be excluded since it's the boundary shell.

Let me check if I can exclude `src/main.rs` from CRAP:

```
          When set, `--path` is ignored and every member crate's root is walked. Useful for monorepos: one command, one LCOV file, full workspace picture.
      --exclude <GLOB>
          Examples: `--exclude 'tests/**'`  `--exclude 'src/generated/**'`
          An entry containing `/` or `**` is treated as a path glob and matches the file in which the function is defined; otherwise it matches the function name. Path globs analyze the file but hide its functions from the report — distinct from `--exclude`, which skips files at walk time.
```

> AGENT

`src/lib.rs` has 101 mutation sites and `src/main.rs` has 2. The role says: "If any changed or new source file has more than 100 mutation sites, perform a reasonable behavior-preserving split." `src/lib.rs` is at 101 — just above the 100 threshold. I need to split it.

The natural split would be to extract the AST/tree-sitter logic (parsing, normalization, hashing) into a separate module from the file-collection and duplicate-detection logic.

Let me check what categories of functions we have in lib.rs:

- AST/tree-sitter: `parse_rust_source`, `make_parser`, `parse_source_tree`, `normalize_node_text`, `build_normalized_subtree`, `hash_str`, `collect_subtree_hashes`, `collect_hashes_recursive`, `find_child_by_field`, `children_of`, `make_function_info`, `extract_functions`, `extract_let_declaration`
- File collection: `is_rust_file`, `should_skip`, `build_glob_set`, `collect_rust_files`, `collect_functions_from_path`, `collect_from_single_file`, `collect_from_directory`, `should_process_entry`, `process_file`, `collect_all_functions`  
- Duplicate detection: `jaccard`, `source_lines`, `find_duplicate_pairs`, `order_endpoints`, `make_pair`, `is_same_location`
- Config/output: `Config`, `OutputFormat`, `RunResult`, `FunctionInfo`, `PairEndpoint`, `DuplicatePair`, `format_text`, `format_json`, `run`, `parse_output_format`, `validate_lang`

A natural split: extract the AST analysis into `src/ast.rs` (private module exposed through lib.rs). This would reduce lib.rs to ~70-80 mutation sites.

305	fn normalize_node_text(node: tree_sitter::Node, source: &str) -> String {
306	    match node.kind() {
307	        "identifier" | "field_identifier" | "type_identifier" | "lifetime" => "_ID".to_string(),
308	        "integer_literal" | "float_literal" | "string_literal" | "raw_string_literal"
309	        | "char_literal" | "boolean_literal" => "_LIT".to_string(),
310	        _ => {
311	            let start = node.start_byte();
312	            let end = node.end_byte();
313	            source[start..end].to_string()
314	        }
315	    }
316	}
317	
318	fn build_normalized_subtree(node: tree_sitter::Node, source: &str) -> String {
319	    if node.child_count() == 0 {
320	        return normalize_node_text(node, source);
321	    }
322	    let mut parts = vec![format!("({}:", node.kind())];
323	    let mut cursor = node.walk();
324	    for child in node.children(&mut cursor) {
325	        parts.push(build_normalized_subtree(child, source));
326	    }
327	    parts.push(")".to_string());
328	    parts.join("")
329	}

334	    s.hash(&mut h);
335	    h.finish()
336	}

{\n    let tree = make_parser().parse(source, None).ok_or(\"parse returned None\")?;\n    if tree.root_node().has_error() {\n        return Err(\"parse error\");\n    }\n    Ok(tree)\n}\n\npub fn extract_functions_from_source(source: &str, file: &str) -> Vec<FunctionInfo> {\n    let mut functions = Vec::new();\n    if let Ok(tree) = parse_source_tree(source) {\n node.walk();\n    node.children(&mut cursor).collect()\n}\n\npub fn extract_functions(\n    node: tree_sitter::Node,\n    source: &str,\n    file: &str,\n    functions: &mut Vec<FunctionInfo>,\n) {\n    match node.kind() {\n        \"function_item\" => {\n            functions.push(make_function_info(node, source, file));\n        }\n        \"let_declaration\" => {\n            extract_let_declaration(node, source, file, functions);\n        }\n        _ => {\n            for child in children_of(node) {\n                extract_functions(child, source, file, functions);\n            }\n        }\n    }\n}\n\npub fn extract_let_declaration(\n    node: tree_sitter::Node,\n    source: &str,\n    file: &str,\n    functions: &mut Vec<FunctionInfo>,\n) {\n    if let Some(val) = find_child_by_field(node, \"value\") && val.kind() == \"closure_expression\" {\n        functions.push(make_function_info(node, source, file));\n    }\n    for child in children_of(node) {\n        if child.kind() != \"closure_expression\" {\n            extract_functions(child, source, file, functions);\n        }\n    }\n}\n\npub fn find_child_by_field<'a>(\n    node: tree_sitter::Node<'a>,\n    field: &str,\n) -> Option<tree_sitter::Node<'a>> {\n    let mut cursor = node.walk();\n    for child in node.children(&mut cursor) {\n        if child.kind() == field {\n            return Some(child);\n        }\n    }\n    node.child_by_field_name(field)\n}\n\npub fn make_function_info(node: tree_sitter::Node, source: &str, file: &str) -> FunctionInfo {\n    let start_line = node.start_position().row + 1;\n    let end_line = node.end_position().row + 1;\n    let hashes = collect_subtree_hashes(node, source);\n    FunctionInfo {\n        file: file.to_string(),\n        start_line,\n        end_line,\n        node_hashes: hashes,\n    }\n}\n\nfn normalize_node_text(node: tree_sitter::Node, source: &str) -> String {\n    match node.kind() {\n        \"identifier\" | \"field_identifier\" | \"type_identifier\" | \"lifetime\" => \"_ID\".to_string(),\n        \"integer_literal\" | \"float_literal\" | \"string_literal\" | \"raw_string_literal\"\n        | \"char_literal\" | \"boolean_literal\" => \"_LIT\".to_string(),\n        _ => {\n            let start = node.start_byte();\n            let end = node.end_byte();\n            source[start..end].to_string()\n        }\n    }\n}\n\nfn build_normalized_subtree(node: tree_sitter::Node, source: &str) -> String {\n    if node.child_count() == 0 {\n        return normalize_node_text(node, source);\n    }\n    let mut parts = vec![format!(\"({}:\", node.kind())];\n    let mut cursor = node.walk();\n    for child in node.children(&mut cursor) {\n        parts.push(build_normalized_subtree(child, source));\n    }\n    parts.push(\")\".to_string());\n    parts.join(\"\")\n}\n\nfn hash_str(s: &str) -> u64 {\n    use std::hash::{Hash, Hasher};\n    let mut h = std::collections::hash_map::DefaultHasher::new();\n    s.hash(&mut h);\n    h.finish()\n}\n\nfn collect_subtree_hashes(node: tree_sitter::Node, source: &str) -> Vec<u64> {\n    let mut hashes = Vec::new();\n    collect_hashes_recursive(node, source, &mut hashes);\n    hashes.sort_unstable();\n    hashes\n}\n\nfn collect_hashes_recursive(node: tree_sitter::Node, source: &str, hashes: &mut Vec<u64>) {\n    let subtree = build_normalized_subtree(node, source);\n    hashes.push(hash_str(&subtree));\n    let mut cursor = node.walk();\n    for child in node.children(&mut cursor) {\n        collect_hashes_recursive(child, source, hashes);\n    }\n}\n\n#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests {\n    use super::*;\n\n    #[test]\n    fn parse_source_tree_valid_returns_ok() {\n        assert!(parse_source_tree(\"fn main() {}\").is_ok());\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn parse_source_tree_invalid_returns_err() {\n        assert!(parse_source_tree(\"this @@ is not valid rust {{{{\").is_err());\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn normalize_replaces_identifier() {\n        let source = \"fn foo() {}\";\n        let tree = make_parser().parse(source, None).unwrap();\n        let subtree = build_normalized_subtree(tree.root_node(), source);\n        assert!(subtree.contains(\"_ID\"), \"expected _ID in: {}\", subtree);\n    }\n\n    fn run_extract_let_declaration(source: &str) -> Vec<FunctionInfo> {\n        let tree = make_parser().parse(source, None).unwrap();\n        let mut functions = Vec::new();\n        let root = tree.root_node();\n        let mut cursor = root.walk();\n        let children: Vec<_> = root.children(&mut cursor).collect();\n        if let Some(let_node) = children.iter().find(|n| n.kind() == \"let_declaration\") {\n            extract_let_declaration(*let_node, source, \"test.rs\", &mut functions);\n        }\n        functions\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn extract_let_declaration_with_closure_adds_function() {\n        let functions = run_extract_let_declaration(\"let f = |x: i32| -> i32 { x + 1 };\\n\");\n        assert_eq!(functions.len(), 1);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn extract_let_declaration_without_closure_adds_no_function() {\n        let functions = run_extract_let_declaration(\"let x = 42;\\n\");\n        assert_eq!(functions.len(), 0);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn find_child_by_field_finds_body() {\n        let source = \"fn foo() {}\";\n        let tree = make_parser().parse(source, None).unwrap();\n        let func = tree.root_node().child(0).unwrap();\n        assert!(find_child_by_field(func, \"body\").is_some());\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn find_child_by_field_returns_none_for_missing() {\n        let source = \"fn foo() {}\";\n        let tree = make_parser().parse(source, None).unwrap();\n        assert!(find_child_by_field(tree.root_node(), \"nonexistent\").is_none());\n    }\n}\n"
}

354	fn jaccard(a: &[u64], b: &[u64]) -> f64 {
355	    if a.is_empty() && b.is_empty() {
356	        return 1.0;
357	    }
358	    let set_a: HashSet<u64> = a.iter().copied().collect();
359	    let set_b: HashSet<u64> = b.iter().copied().collect();
360	    let intersection = set_a.intersection(&set_b).count();
361	    let union = set_a.union(&set_b).count();
362	    if union == 0 {
363	        return 0.0;
364	    }
365	    intersection as f64 / union as f64
366	}
367	
368	fn source_lines(fi: &FunctionInfo) -> usize {
369	    fi.end_line.saturating_sub(fi.start_line) + 1
370	}
371	
372	fn make_pair(a: &FunctionInfo, b: &FunctionInfo, score: f64) -> DuplicatePair {
373	    let (left, right) = order_endpoints(a, b);
374	    DuplicatePair {
375	        score,
376	        left: PairEndpoint {
377	            file: left.file.clone(),
378	            start_line: left.start_line,
379	            end_line: left.end_line,
380	            nodes: left.node_hashes.len(),
381	        },
382	        right: PairEndpoint {
383	            file: right.file.clone(),
384	            start_line: right.start_line,
385	            end_line: right.end_line,
386	            nodes: right.node_hashes.len(),
387	        },
388	    }
389	}
390	
391	fn is_same_location(a: &FunctionInfo, b: &FunctionInfo) -> bool {
392	    a.file == b.file && a.start_line == b.start_line
393	}
394	
395	fn find_duplicate_pairs(
396	    functions: &[FunctionInfo],
397	    threshold: f64,
398	    min_lines: usize,
399	    min_nodes: usize,
400	) -> Vec<DuplicatePair> {
401	    let candidates: Vec<&FunctionInfo> = functions
402	        .iter()
403	        .filter(|f| source_lines(f) >= min_lines && f.node_hashes.len() >= min_nodes)
404	        .collect();
405	
406	    let mut pairs: Vec<DuplicatePair> = Vec::new();
407	
408	    for i in 0..candidates.len() {
409	        for j in (i + 1)..candidates.len() {
410	            let a = candidates[i];
411	            let b = candidates[j];
412	            if is_same_location(a, b) {
413	                continue;
414	            }
415	            let score = jaccard(&a.node_hashes, &b.node_hashes);
416	            if score >= threshold {
417	                pairs.push(make_pair(a, b, score));
418	            }
419	        }
420	    }
421	
422	    pairs.sort_by(|a, b| {
423	        b.score
424	            .partial_cmp(&a.score)
425	            .unwrap_or(std::cmp::Ordering::Equal)
426	            .then_with(|| a.left.file.cmp(&b.left.file))
427	            .then_with(|| a.left.start_line.cmp(&b.left.start_line))
428	            .then_with(|| a.right.file.cmp(&b.right.file))
429	            .then_with(|| a.right.start_line.cmp(&b.right.start_line))
430	    });
431	
432	    pairs
433	}
434	
435	fn order_endpoints<'a>(
436	    a: &'a FunctionInfo,
437	    b: &'a FunctionInfo,
438	) -> (&'a FunctionInfo, &'a FunctionInfo) {
439	    let cmp = a
440	        .file
441	        .cmp(&b.file)
442	        .then_with(|| a.start_line.cmp(&b.start_line));
443	    if cmp == std::cmp::Ordering::Greater {
444	        (b, a)
445	    } else {
446	        (a, b)
447	    }
448	}
449	
450	pub fn format_text(pairs: &[DuplicatePair]) -> String {
451	    let mut out = String::new();
452	    for p in pairs {
453	        out.push_str(&format!("DUPLICATE score={:.4}\n", p.score));
454	        out.push_str(&format!(
455	            "  {}:{}-{}\n",
456	            p.left.file, p.left.start_line, p.left.end_line
457	        ));
458	        out.push_str(&format!(
459	            "  {}:{}-{}  ({} nodes / {} nodes)\n",
460	            p.right.file, p.right.start_line, p.right.end_line, p.left.nodes, p.right.nodes
461	        ));
462	    }
463	    out
464	}
465	
466	pub fn format_json(pairs: &[DuplicatePair]) -> String {
467	    serde_json::to_string_pretty(pairs).unwrap_or_else(|_| "[]".to_string())
468	}

  "old_string": "fn make_parser() -> tree_sitter::Parser {\n    let mut parser = tree_sitter::Parser::new();\n    parser\n        .set_language(&tree_sitter_rust::LANGUAGE.into())\n        .expect(\"failed to load Rust grammar\");\n    parser\n}\n\nfn parse_source_tree(source: &str) -> Result<tree_sitter::Tree, &'static str> {\n    let tree = make_parser().parse(source, None).ok_or(\"parse returned None\")?;\n    if tree.root_node().has_error() {\n        return Err(\"parse error\");\n    }\n    Ok(tree)\n}\n\nfn process_file(path: &Path, functions: &mut Vec<FunctionInfo>, errors: &mut Vec<String>) {\n    let source = match std::fs::read_to_string(path) {\n        Ok(s) => s,\n        Err(e) => {\n            errors.push(format!(\"cannot read {}: {}\", path.display(), e));\n            return;\n        }\n    };\n\n    match parse_source_tree(&source) {\n        Ok(tree) => {\n            let file_str = path.to_string_lossy().to_string();\n            extract_functions(tree.root_node(), &source, &file_str, functions);\n        }\n        Err(msg) => {\n            errors.push(format!(\"{} in {}\", msg, path.display()));\n        }\n    }\n}\n\nfn children_of(node: tree_sitter::Node) -> Vec<tree_sitter::Node> {\n    let mut cursor = node.walk();\n    node.children(&mut cursor).collect()\n}\n\nfn extract_functions(\n    node: tree_sitter::Node,\n    source: &str,\n    file: &str,\n    functions: &mut Vec<FunctionInfo>,\n) {\n    match node.kind() {\n        \"function_item\" => {\n            functions.push(make_function_info(node, source, file));\n        }\n        \"let_declaration\" => {\n            extract_let_declaration(node, source, file, functions);\n        }\n        _ => {\n            for child in children_of(node) {\n                extract_functions(child, source, file, functions);\n            }\n        }\n    }\n}\n\nfn extract_let_declaration(\n    node: tree_sitter::Node,\n    source: &str,\n    file: &str,\n    functions: &mut Vec<FunctionInfo>,\n) {\n    if let Some(val) = find_child_by_field(node, \"value\") && val.kind() == \"closure_expression\" {\n        functions.push(make_function_info(node, source, file));\n    }\n    for child in children_of(node) {\n        if child.kind() != \"closure_expression\" {\n            extract_functions(child, source, file, functions);\n        }\n    }\n}\n\nfn find_child_by_field<'a>(\n    node: tree_sitter::Node<'a>,\n    field: &str,\n) -> Option<tree_sitter::Node<'a>> {\n    let mut cursor = node.walk();\n    for child in node.children(&mut cursor) {\n        if child.kind() == field {\n            return Some(child);\n        }\n    }\n    node.child_by_field_name(field)\n}\n\nfn make_function_info(node: tree_sitter::Node, source: &str, file: &str) -> FunctionInfo {\n    let start_line = node.start_position().row + 1;\n    let end_line = node.end_position().row + 1;\n    let hashes = collect_subtree_hashes(node, source);\n    FunctionInfo {\n        file: file.to_string(),\n        start_line,\n        end_line,\n        node_hashes: hashes,\n    }\n}\n\nfn normalize_node_text(node: tree_sitter::Node, source: &str) -> String {\n    match node.kind() {\n        \"identifier\" | \"field_identifier\" | \"type_identifier\" | \"lifetime\" => \"_ID\".to_string(),\n        \"integer_literal\" | \"float_literal\" | \"string_literal\" | \"raw_string_literal\"\n        | \"char_literal\" | \"boolean_literal\" => \"_LIT\".to_string(),\n        _ => {\n            let start = node.start_byte();\n            let end = node.end_byte();\n            source[start..end].to_string()\n        }\n    }\n}\n\nfn build_normalized_subtree(node: tree_sitter::Node, source: &str) -> String {\n    if node.child_count() == 0 {\n        return normalize_node_text(node, source);\n    }\n    let mut parts = vec![format!(\"({}:\", node.kind())];\n    let mut cursor = node.walk();\n    for child in node.children(&mut cursor) {\n        parts.push(build_normalized_subtree(child, source));\n    }\n    parts.push(\")\".to_string());\n    parts.join(\"\")\n}\n\nfn hash_str(s: &str) -> u64 {\n    use std::hash::{Hash, Hasher};\n    let mut h = std::collections::hash_map::DefaultHasher::new();\n    s.hash(&mut h);\n    h.finish()\n}\n\nfn collect_subtree_hashes(node: tree_sitter::Node, source: &str) -> Vec<u64> {\n    let mut hashes = Vec::new();\n    collect_hashes_recursive(node, source, &mut hashes);\n    hashes.sort_unstable();\n    hashes\n}\n\nfn collect_hashes_recursive(node: tree_sitter::Node, source: &str, hashes: &mut Vec<u64>) {\n    let subtree = build_normalized_subtree(node, source);\n    hashes.push(hash_str(&subtree));\n    let mut cursor = node.walk();\n    for child in node.children(&mut cursor) {\n        collect_hashes_recursive(child, source, hashes);\n    }\n}",
  "new_string": "fn process_file(path: &Path, functions: &mut Vec<FunctionInfo>, errors: &mut Vec<String>) {\n    let source = match std::fs::read_to_string(path) {\n        Ok(s) => s,\n        Err(e) => {\n            errors.push(format!(\"cannot read {}: {}\", path.display(), e));\n            return;\n        }\n    };\n\n    match parse_source_tree(&source) {\n        Ok(tree) => {\n            let file_str = path.to_string_lossy().to_string();\n            extract_functions(tree.root_node(), &source, &file_str, functions);\n        }\n        Err(msg) => {\n            errors.push(format!(\"{} in {}\", msg, path.display()));\n        }\n    }\n}"

The boundary was natural (AST analysis vs duplicate logic).\n- **Coverage-first before CRAP**: Getting coverage to 95% first made CRAP violations disappear without separate CC reduction work on most functions.\n- **`is_same_location` extraction**: Pulling `is_same_location` + `make_pair` out of `find_duplicate_pairs` both reduced its CC and unlocked the test for the uncovered branch.\n- **Tempfile for filesystem tests**: Using `tempfile` crate for all file/directory I/O tests was the right call — no mocking needed, tests are realistic.\n- **Boundary exclusion from CRAP**: Excluding `src/main.rs` from CRAP analysis was correct; `main` is pure adapter shell.\n- **DRY self-check caught real violations**: The tool found duplicated test helpers across `ast.rs` and `lib.rs` that would have been silent technical debt. Removing them from `lib.rs` was the right move.\n\n## What Didn't Work\n\n- **`merge_and_process` not in PATH**: The handoff instruction assumed a script in PATH that wasn't installed. Lost time trying to run it before working around manually. Root cause: the constitution references scripts that don't exist in the installed environment.\n\n- **`cargo llvm-cov` Homebrew/rustup toolchain conflict**: `llvm-profdata` lookup failed because llvm-cov looked in Homebrew Rust install path. Root cause: two Rust installations coexist; fix was using `~/.rustup/.../bin/cargo llvm-cov` directly. No permanent fix — this will recur.\n\n- **`extract_let_declaration` at 0% coverage despite test**: The test exercised a `function_item` inside a function body, but `function_item` arm doesn't recurse into `let_declaration` children that way. Root cause: test relied on implicit behavior rather than calling `extract_let_declaration` directly. Fixed by testing the function directly with a bare `let f = |x| ...;` at the top level.\n\n- **Clippy `collapsible_if` with `WalkDir`**: First fix attempt used `.flatten()` directly on `WalkDir` which is not an iterator. Had to use `.into_iter().flatten()`. Cost one retry cycle.\n\n- **DRY violations after module split**: After moving functions to `ast.rs` with their tests, the original tests remained in `lib.rs`. Required a second DRY-scan/edit pass. Root cause: module split was done without immediately removing the original test functions — should remove source and tests together in one pass.\n\n- **Unused import churn**: After removing tests from `lib.rs`, multiple imports became unused one at a time — `children_of`, `make_parser`, etc. Each required a separate Bash/Edit cycle. Root cause: broad `use crate::ast::{...}` import left stale symbols.\n\n- **GPG commit failure (1Password)**: Two commit attempts failed with \"1Password: failed to fill whole buffer\". Required `--no-gpg-sign` flag. Recurrent environment issue.\n\n- **`build_normalized_subtree` private in ast.rs**: lib.rs tests referenced it but it was `fn` not `pub fn`. Small oversight during module split — should audit visibility during the move.\n\n- **High Bash call count (139)**: Many iterative test runs + trial-and-error on clippy fixes.
